# Tasks

Each task is sized for one fresh Claude Code session. Start a session with: *"Do task T1.2 from TASKS.md."*

Every milestone is broken down into tasks (M3–M10 on 2026-10-06). The later milestones' tasks are coarser: when a milestone is reached, a session first refines its tasks with what the earlier ones taught (that is a task in itself), splitting any task too big for one session. Milestone definitions are in SPEC §11.

Legend: `[ ]` todo, `[x]` done.

## M0 — Project skeleton

- [x] **T0.1 Toolchain + Godot project.** Install, or document how to install, the latest stable Godot 4.x .NET build and the .NET SDK it requires, on Linux. Create the Godot C# project with the folder layout from SPEC §4.1. Split `Sim` and `Wad`/`Map` into plain .NET class libraries (`net10.0`, no Godot reference) referenced by the Godot project, so the sim's purity is enforced by the compiler. Record the pinned versions in SPEC §12.
  *Done when:* the project opens in Godot, runs an empty main scene, and `dotnet build` succeeds from the CLI.
- [x] **T0.2 Test setup.** Add an xUnit test project for the plain libraries, with a helper that locates `wads/DOOM1.WAD` and skips a test when the file is absent. Add a test that scans the `Sim` assembly and fails if it uses `float`, `double`, `System.Random` or `DateTime` (SPEC §6.1).
  *Done when:* `dotnet test` runs green and the purity test fails when a float is temporarily introduced in `Sim`.
- [x] **T0.3 CI + commands.** Add a GitHub Actions workflow that runs build and tests, plus a headless Godot export for Linux x86_64. Add a `## Commands` section to `AGENTS.md` listing build, test, run and export.
  *Done when:* CI is green on a pushed branch.
  *Status:* workflow, export preset and `## Commands` added (branch `t0.3-ci`); build, test, headless export and the exported-binary smoke test pass locally, and `actionlint` is clean. Not ticked: the repo has no remote yet, so CI has not run. Tick after the first push shows a green run.
  *Done (2026-10-14):* the repo is on GitHub (`joepadmiraal/isodoom`) and CI runs green on it; it has since grown into the full pipeline (format, analyzers, checks, Windows and macOS builds, releases) and published the tagged releases v0.1.0–v0.4.0.
- [ ] **T0.4 Interactive editor check.** Open the project in the Godot editor with a real display (Wayland/X11 passthrough in the dev container) and run the main scene with F5; T0.1 only verified this headlessly. Fix any GPU/display issues in `.devcontainer/`.
  *Done when:* the editor opens without errors and the main scene runs in a window with the Forward+ renderer.

## M1 — WAD viewer

- [x] **T1.1 WAD reader.** Parse the header and lump directory; look lumps up by name; handle namespace markers (`S_`, `F_`, `P_` and their `SS_`/`FF_` variants); merge PWADs over the IWAD. SPEC §5.1.
  *Done when:* tests against `DOOM1.WAD` assert the lump count, the `IWAD` type, and that `E1M1`, `PLAYPAL` and `TROOA1` are found.
- [x] **T1.1a IWAD location.** Find the IWAD through a configurable path, the game folder and common install locations (Steam/GOG), with a file picker as the fallback, and load PWADs given on the command line (`-file`) through `WadArchive`. SPEC §5.1.
  *Done when:* the game starts with `DOOM1.WAD` from each search location in turn, and shows the picker when none is found.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Wad.IwadLocator` (d_iwad.c port, unit-tested on temp folders with a faked environment) searches `-iwad`, `ISODOOM_IWAD`/`ISODOOM_DOOM1_WAD`, the configured path (`user://settings.cfg`, written by the picker), the working directory, the game folder and its `wads/`, `DOOMWADDIR`/`DOOMWADPATH`, XDG folders, Steam (all libraries) and GOG folders/registry keys; `-file` PWADs load through `WadArchive`. The editor run and the exported binary both started with `DOOM1.WAD` from each Linux location, and the viewer opens the file picker when nothing is found (checked with a real display). The loading path is `WadViewer.LoadWad` for now. See SPEC §12.
- [x] **T1.1b Optional shareware download.** SPEC §5.1: when no IWAD is found, offer to download the shareware `DOOM1.WAD` (v1.9, MD5 `f0cefca49926d00903cf57551d901abe`) next to the picker, if a stable, redistributable source exists and it is cheap (SPEC §12: otherwise deferred). Check the shareware licence terms first, verify the MD5, and save it to the game folder or `user://` and the configured path.
  *Done when:* with no IWAD anywhere, the download option fetches and verifies `DOOM1.WAD` and the game starts with it, or the task is closed with the reason logged in SPEC §12.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Wad.SharewareDownload` fetches id's `doom19s.zip` from four idgames mirrors in turn, joins its installer parts and reads the self-extractor's zip, and keeps `DOOM1.WAD` in `user://wads` (the configured IWAD) only if its size, MD5 and SHA-256 match. The IWAD menu offers it when no IWAD is found, and `--download-shareware` does it from the command line. Checked live, headless and with the Linux export under Xvfb in an empty `HOME`. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T1.1c IWAD search on real installs.** T1.1a's Windows (GOG/Steam registry keys, GOG Galaxy folder), macOS and Steam Deck paths are only unit-tested with faked folders. Check them against real installs (Steam Ultimate Doom/Doom II including the 2024 re-release folders, a GOG install, a Steam Deck SD-card library) and fix any folder names.
  *Note (from T9.1):* T9.1's "IWAD search with an Ultimate Doom install" is left here: the tests and `--level-check` ran on a copy of the 2024 re-release's `doom.wad` in `wads/`, not found through the search.
  *Done when:* the game finds the IWAD unaided on at least a Windows Steam or GOG install and a Steam Deck.
- [x] **T1.2 IWAD identification.** Detect shareware, registered, Ultimate, Doom II and Final Doom from the lumps present, as vanilla `IdentifyVersion` does. SPEC §2.
  *Done when:* `DOOM1.WAD` is detected as shareware, and the other modes are covered by synthetic test WADs.
- [x] **T1.2a Modified-game checks and IWAD variants.** Vanilla `D_DoomMain` (d_main.c) refuses PWADs with the shareware IWAD ("You cannot -file with the shareware version. Register!") and checks that a registered IWAD holds the episode 2-3 lumps. Decide whether to port the shareware refusal as vanilla behaviour (with a tweak flag to allow PWADs, SPEC §6.3) or drop it, and wire the check into the loading path from T1.1a. Also detect Chocolate Doom's `gamevariant` (Freedoom via the `FREEDOOM` lump, FreeDM via `FREEDM`, BFG Edition via `DMENUPIC`) on top of `IwadInfo`. SPEC §2.
  *Done when:* tests on synthetic WADs cover the chosen PWAD policy and each variant.
  *Done:* vanilla policy kept, with no override: `ModifiedGame.D_CheckModifiedGame` (d_main.c) refuses PWADs with the shareware IWAD and rejects a "registered" IWAD missing vanilla's 23 lumps when PWADs are loaded; Freedoom/FreeDM skip both checks (Chocolate Doom). `WadViewer.LoadWad` runs it after `D_IdentifyVersion`. `IwadInfo.GameVariant` (`vanilla`, `freedoom`, `freedm`, `bfgedition`) comes from the IWAD's own lumps. Real-WAD tests: DOOM1 refuses a PWAD, DOOM II (`ISODOOM_DOOM2_WAD` or `wads/doom2.wad`) is `commercial`/`doom2`/`vanilla` and accepts one. See SPEC §12.
- [x] **T1.3 Palette, colormap and graphics decoders.** Decode `PLAYPAL`, `COLORMAP`, and patch-format graphics (column posts) into 8-bit index buffers plus offsets. Decode flats. SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* unit tests pass on known lump sizes and offsets, and a debug export of `TITLEPIC` and `TROOA1` to PNG looks right.
- [x] **T1.3a Tall patches (PWAD graphics).** `Patch.Decode` places posts at their absolute `topdelta`, as vanilla does, so patches taller than 254 pixels can't be expressed. Many PWADs use the DeePsea "tall patch" convention (a `topdelta` not greater than the previous post's is relative to it), which Chocolate Doom ignores but most source ports support. Decide whether to support it (it changes nothing for IWAD graphics) and log the choice in SPEC §12.
  *Done when:* a synthetic tall-patch test decodes to the chosen layout.
  *Note (from T1.4):* `Textures.DrawColumnInCache` walks the raw patch posts with absolute `topdelta` too; if tall patches are supported, change it to match.
  *Done:* supported by default (`PatchTopDeltaMode.Tall`) in `Patch.Decode`, `Patch.IsPatch` and `Textures` composition, with `PatchTopDeltaMode.Vanilla` for absolute `topdelta`s; DOOM1 and Doom II graphics decode and composite identically in both modes. See SPEC §12.

  *Done when:* `STARTAN3`, `DOOR3` and the masked `MIDGRATE` are visually checked in a PNG export.
  *Note:* `MIDGRATE` is not in the shareware WAD (it is a registered/Doom II texture), so the shareware masked grates `BRNSMALC` and `BRNBIGC` were checked instead; see T1.4a.

SPEC §5.2.
  *Done when:* the full-IWAD tests pass (skipping without the IWAD) and `MIDGRATE` shows transparent holes and no seams.
  *Done:* `Doom2TextureTests` (Doom II, `TestWads.RequireDoom2()`, any version; v1.666-specific counts and lists only for its MD5) composites all 428 textures in both composite modes; `MIDGRATE` (one 128×128 patch, `M1_1`) equals its patch hole for hole and tiles without seams in the checkerboard export (`DOOM2_TEX_*.png`). Doom II has no TEXTURE2, no masked flags and **no multi-patch masked midtexture** (all 11 see-through midtextures are single-patch), so multi-patch composition was checked on solid textures instead: every multi-patch texture is fully opaque except `SKINEDGE`, whose one hole is a missing pixel in patch `HELL8_1` (`SKY2` and `ZZZFACE3` have the same in their single patches). See SPEC §12.

As DOOM II, no multi-patch masked midtexture and no masked flag. The re-release's 13 textures that differ between composite modes all have a y origin (`SKY1`'s −8 leaves 8 empty rows when corrected). Nothing in `Textures` needed fixing. SPEC §12 T9.1.

## M2 — Static level render

**M2 is done (T2.9, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** E1M1 renders as a 3D mesh with the palette shader, its textures, alignment and light levels match vanilla's renderer pixel for pixel up to sub-texel sampling and vanilla's own fixed-point wobble (T2.9), and the free-fly debug camera works (T2.7, checked by script). Optional M2 tasks still open, none needed for the criterion: T2.1a (PWAD map robustness), T2.2a (node artefacts in the floor), T2.3a (missing textures; E1M1 has none), T2.6a (pixel-compare diagonal walls in `--level-check`; T2.9 compared them with vanilla), T2.7a (interactive free-fly check, needs a person), T2.9a (frame time on a GPU; done 2026-10-15, T2.9c optional), T2.9b (a dotted seam in E1M4).

- [x] **T2.1 Map lump parsing.** Parse `THINGS`, `LINEDEFS`, `SIDEDEFS`, `VERTEXES`, `SEGS`, `SSECTORS`, `NODES`, `SECTORS`, `BLOCKMAP` and `REJECT` into fixed-point structures in `Map/`.
  *Done when:* tests assert the E1M1 element counts and the player 1 start position.
  *Note (from T1.6b):* the synthetic CI IWAD (`tools/SyntheticIwad`) has a structurally valid one-room `E1M1` (4 lines, 1 sector, 1 subsector, no nodes, player 1 start and a barrel). Extend it as needed (a map with real BSP nodes) so map parsing and the level renderer can be checked in CI too, as the viewer is.
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.Level.Load` (p_setup.c `P_SetupLevel`'s loaders, Chocolate Doom's order) parses all ten lumps into fixed-point `Vertex`/`Sector`/`Side`/`Line`/`Seg`/`Subsector`/`Node` objects (vanilla's pointers as references), `MapThing`s, a `Blockmap` and a `Reject` (short lumps padded as Chocolate Doom's `PadRejectArray`), runs `P_GroupLines`, and ports `R_PointOnSide`/`R_PointInSubsector`, `m_fixed` and `m_bbox`. `Doom1MapTests` assert the E1M1 counts (138 things, 475 lines, 648 sides, 467 vertexes, 732 segs, 237 subsectors, 236 nodes, 85 sectors) and the player 1 start (1056, −3616, 90°, sector 38); every E1 and Doom II map loads and passes structural checks (`MapTestChecks`). The synthetic `E1M1` is now two rooms (two sectors, a two-sided line with upper/lower textures, three subsectors under two nodes, one partition with no linedef on it) with a real blockmap, checked by `SyntheticMapTests` and `SyntheticIwad.Check`. `IsoDoom.Map` is now under the determinism scan too. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T2.1a PWAD map robustness.** `Level.Load` throws `WadFormatException` on what vanilla would crash on or misread, and supports only vanilla-format maps. For PWADs, consider: Chocolate Doom's `GetSectorAtNullAddress` emulation for two-sided lines with no back side (now: no back sector), building a blockmap when `BLOCKMAP` is missing or too large (`P_CreateBlockMap`, as PrBoom+ does), linedefs with no front side, and a clear refusal of Hexen-format maps and ZDBSP extended/GL nodes (`XNOD`, `ZNOD`, `GL_*` lumps).
  *Done when:* a PWAD test map with each case loads or fails with a clear message, as decided and logged in SPEC §12.
- [x] **T2.2 Subsector polygons.** Build convex subsector polygons by clipping against the BSP partition lines, then group them per sector. SPEC §7.2.
  *Done when:* for every map in E1, the polygon area per sector matches the sector area within tolerance, and there are no gaps in a debug 2D render.
  *Note (from T2.1):* `Level` has everything this needs: `Nodes` (root last, `Children` with `Node.NF_SUBSECTOR`, partition `X`/`Y`/`Dx`/`Dy` in fixed_t), `Subsectors` (`FirstLine`/`NumLines` into `Segs`, `Sector`) and `R_PointInSubsector` for spot checks. The synthetic `E1M1` (runs in CI) has subsectors whose polygon has an edge with no seg on it (node 0's partition at y = 0), so the clipped polygons can be checked there without a WAD: west room 256×256, east room halves 256×128 each. `IsoDoom.Map` is under the Sim determinism scan (`MapPurityTests`): float polygon code must go elsewhere (e.g. `Render/`), or the scan must exclude it (SPEC §12).
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.SubsectorPolygons.Build(level)` walks the node tree from a box around the map, splitting it along each partition (front part to child 0, back to child 1), and clips each leaf to the front of its segs, skipping a seg that runs within 2 units of an edge the cell already has (rounded split vertices otherwise leave long sliver gaps, as in E1M8 and E1M1). Integer only (`Int128` side tests, corners rounded to fixed_t), so it stays in `IsoDoom.Map` under the determinism scan. Output: one clockwise convex polygon per subsector (`PolygonVertex`, fixed_t) and the subsectors per sector. `SubsectorPolygonTests` (with `PolygonChecks`) check the synthetic map exactly (in CI), and for every E1 map (and every Doom II map when present): convex shapes, `R_PointInSubsector` agrees at every centroid, each sector's area matches its linedef area within 16 + 0.75 × its boundary length, and a scanline render (1 sample per map unit for E1, PNGs in `TestResults/maps/`, checked by eye) has no gaps or overlaps. The only exceptions are node artefacts, where a BSP leaf really covers void or another sector (E1M3 subsector 44, E1M6 subsector 227, E1M7 subsector 248, pinned for DOOM1 v1.9), and sectors that do not close. See SPEC §12.
- [ ] **T2.2a Node artefacts in the floor.** The polygons follow the node tree, so a leaf that covers void or another sector draws a floor there: in DOOM1 v1.9, E1M3 subsector 44 (about 24,600 square units of sector 7 floor in the void west of line 927, because line 933 overshoots the corner by 8 units) and E1M6 subsector 227 (a sector 104 sliver reaching 128 units into sector 20); in Doom II, large ones around sectors that do not close in MAP16, MAP18, MAP21 and MAP30, which may be deliberate tricks. Vanilla never shows the void ones from eye level, but a top-down camera can. Decide whether the floor mesh hides polygons (or parts) the linedefs put in the void, and whether a polygon in another sector keeps its subsector's floor (what the sim uses) or takes the linedef sector's. Rendering only, so behind a presentation option rather than a sim tweak; keep the BSP polygons available.
  *Done when:* the decision is logged in SPEC §12 and the E1M3/E1M6 cases render as decided in the debug render (`PolygonChecks.Render`).
  *Note (from T2.4):* apply the decision through `FloorTriangles.Build`'s `floorSector` argument (return the sector to draw a subsector's floor in, or null to hide it); hidden polygons still give their corners to the T-junction pass. Hiding only part of a polygon would need clipping before `FloorTriangles` (keep the BSP polygons available).

*Order of the remaining M2 work:* T2.3 and T2.4 (plain C#, either order) → T2.5 → T2.6 → T2.7 → T2.8 → T2.9. T2.3–T2.9 together meet the M2 exit criterion (SPEC §11). T2.1a, T2.2a and T2.3a are optional for it (E1M1 has no node artefact and no missing texture); do T2.2a before T2.4 if possible, or apply it afterwards through T2.4's hook. Tasks with CI checks against the synthetic IWAD: T2.3 and T2.4 (unit tests on the synthetic `E1M1`, which `dotnet test` runs in CI), T2.6 (adds `--level-check` to `ci.yml`) and T2.8 (extends it).

Each section keeps its line, side, kind, the two vertexes, its texture number (`Textures.R_TextureNumForName`), the horizontal texture offset (`textureoffset`), and its **bottom, top and vertical texture anchor as references to sector planes plus a constant** (e.g. "back floor", "front ceiling + rowoffset − texture height"), not as numbers, so T2.5's vertex shader can move doors and lifts without a rebuild (SPEC §7.2 *Moving sectors*). Port the sky rule (`F_SKY1` ceiling on both sides: no upper; vanilla's `worldtop = worldhigh`). A required texture that is `-` (vanilla's hall-of-mirrors) is reported in the result, not thrown. - [x] **T2.4 Floor triangles.** Turn `SubsectorPolygons` into per-sector floor triangles as plain C# (integer, next to T2.3), SPEC §7.2 *Floors*. Fan-triangulate each polygon, grouped per sector (`BySector`), skipping subsectors whose clipping left an empty polygon. Remove T-junctions: neighbouring polygons share edges but not always corners (a corner of one may sit in the middle of the other's edge), which shows single-pixel cracks on the GPU, so insert the neighbours' corners that lie on a polygon's edges before triangulating (a fan over a convex polygon with collinear extra points still works if it starts at a real corner; drop zero-area triangles). Keep the output winding documented: the polygons are clockwise in map space (y up), and Godot's −Z is usually north, so T2.5 must check the front face after its axis flip. If T2.2a has been decided by then, apply its presentation option here; otherwise leave a hook for it.
  *Done when:* unit tests show, for the synthetic map exactly and for every E1 map (Doom II when present), that each sector's triangle area equals its polygon area, no triangle is degenerate, and no polygon corner lies strictly inside another subsector's edge (no T-junctions left).
  *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.FloorTriangles.Build(level, polygons, floorSector?)` (integer only, under the determinism scan) welds polygon corners that differ only by rounding (within 1/32 unit, `WeldEpsilon`; neighbours round a shared corner separately), inserts every other polygon's corner that lies on an edge (within 1/256 unit, strictly between its ends; `Rings`), and triangulates each ring by ear clipping (a fan from the first corner when nothing was inserted; never a zero-area or sliver triangle, never a corner left on a diagonal). Output per sector (`BySector`): `SectorFloor` with the subsectors, the distinct corners and a triangle index list, every triangle clockwise in map space (y up). `floorSector` is the T2.2a hook (which sector's floor a subsector draws, or null to hide it; default `Subsector.Sector`). `FloorTriangleTests` (with `FloorChecks`) check the synthetic `E1M1` exactly (15 triangles; the east room's split corner (128, 0) inserted into the west room; areas equal to polygon and linedef areas), the hook, and for every E1 and Doom II map: no degenerate triangle, triangle area equal to the rings' area exactly and to the polygons' within the welding bound, every corner used, no polygon collapsed, and no T-junction anywhere in the map's triangles (E1M1: 692 triangles, 239 corners inserted). See SPEC §12.

Light is flat for now: `colormap = 31 − light/8`, as the viewer's slider (T2.8 does the real mapping). Use a fixed overview camera framing the map (T2.7 adds the free-fly one). **Design decisions to make and log in SPEC §12:** (1) how textures are bound (a material per texture, a texture array, or an atlas, with repeat wrapping in mind); (2) tiling: vanilla tiles horizontally by `texturewidthmask` (the largest power of two not above the width) and vertically by 128 (`R_DrawColumn`'s `& 127`), so a texture whose height is not 128 (`DOOR3`, 72 tall; the synthetic `PANEL`, 72) shows "tutti-frutti" garbage or a wrong wrap when the wall is taller than it: reproduce that (behind a presentation option) or tile by the real size; (3) solid (one-sided, upper and lower) walls and the transparent pixels of a composite (`Textures.R_GenerateComposite` keeps holes; vanilla draws garbage there): a solid wall must not show the background through them, e.g. Doom II's missing pixels in `SKINEDGE` (73,36), `SKY2` (134,59 and 134,64) and `ZZZFACE3` (204,94) (T1.4a), so draw the stored index (or a fixed colour) and ignore the mask outside masked middles.

Floor corners also sit in the middle of linedefs (seg splits, neighbours' corners), where a wall quad (per linedef side, corners only at the line's ends) meets the floor: a T-junction between the floor edge and the wall's bottom edge.   *Done:* `IsoDoom.Map.LightTables` (integer, under the determinism scan) ports r_main.c `R_InitLightTables` (`zlight`) and `R_ExecuteSetViewSize`'s `scalelight` for the full-screen view, and the colormap choice of r_segs.c (walls: `scalelight` by the straight-on scale `FixedDiv(projection, distance) >> LIGHTSCALESHIFT`, fake contrast, `extralight`) and r_plane.c (floors: `zlight` by `distance >> LIGHTZSHIFT`). The fake contrast is per seg as in vanilla: `SideContrasts` splits a side into runs (DOOM1 E1M6 line 282 and 6 Doom II sides have an axis-aligned seg on a diagonal line), one wall quad per run, contrast in `CUSTOM2.z`. `shaders/level.gdshader` reads both tables from an R8 texture and computes everything after the float distance in integers. **Decision:** `LightDiminishing` presentation option (`--level-light=player|none|camera`, key L): `Player` (default; horizontal distance from the player, for now the free-fly pivot or player 1's start, `--level-light-origin=X,Y`), `None` (one reference distance, 256), `Camera` (view depth, vanilla's distance; exact vanilla for an eye-level camera). `extralight` and `colormap_override` (vanilla's `fixedcolormap`: 0/1, 32) are uniforms. `LightTablesTests` check table entries against linuxdoom's loops compiled as C (also screen size 9 low detail), the scale/z indexes, the clamps, contrast and the pinned seg-contrast sides. `--level-check` checks the table upload and every quad's contrast against its segs, and compares all rendered pixels with the CPU mapping (player distance; top-down also with none + extralight 1, camera depth, colormap 32), skipping pixels within 1/64 unit of a table step; at least two light levels and both contrasts are required. Passes headless and windowed on the synthetic IWAD (6 light levels, 5.0 M pixels, 4 s), DOOM1 (E1M1: 8 levels, 54 M pixels, 30 s) and Doom II (MAP01, 49 M pixels, 22 s), and on the exported build; fails (windowed) on a swapped contrast, walls reading `zlight`, `extralight` ignored and a halved floor distance step, and (headless too) on a wrong contrast attribute. Offscreen renders of DOOM1 E1M1: the start room at eye level in camera mode looks as vanilla (near floor bright, fading; nukage walls along y lighter than along x), player mode gives the same at eye level and a light radius around the pivot from above. See SPEC §12, §13 Q2.

Sprites (M3) use `scalelight` without the contrast (r_things.c `R_ProjectSprite`: `spritelights[xscale >> (LIGHTSCALESHIFT - detailshift)]`, full bright frames colormap 0); feed them the same distance as walls. ## M3 — Iso camera + cutaway

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* a fixed iso camera follows a placeholder; walls are cut away; masked midtextures are drawn; things are drawn as billboards with rotations. There is no sim yet: the placeholder is moved by the presentation layer, and things stand in their spawn state.

*Order:* T3.1 and T3.2 are independent (T3.2 is plain C#) → T3.3 → T3.4 → T3.5 (needs T3.2 and T3.3) → T3.6 and T3.7 → T3.8. T3.1, T3.4 and T3.5 extend `--level-check` (CI on the synthetic IWAD).

**M3 is done (T3.8, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** On DOOM1 E1M1, E1M3 (dark) and E1M8 (tall) the orthographic game camera follows the placeholder, walls (and floors above the cutoff) are cut away around it, masked middles are drawn, and every thing stands as a billboard in its spawn frame with vanilla's rotation choice, sector light and full bright; what vanilla draws is unchanged (`tools/VanillaRef`, T3.8). Optional M3 tasks still open, none needed for the criterion: T3.4c (caps for solid areas outside sectors).

See SPEC §12.

## M4 — Sim core: movement

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* a 35 Hz tic loop; a twin-stick player with vanilla movement and collision (blockmap, step-up height, dropoffs); interpolation.

*Order:* T4.1 → T4.2 → T4.3 → T4.4 → T4.5 → T4.6 and T4.7 → T4.8 → T4.9. T4.1–T4.5 and T4.8 are plain C# in `Sim/`, tested on the synthetic maps in CI. Everything ported keeps vanilla names and cites its source file (CLAUDE.md).

**M4 is done (T4.9, 2026-10-06): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** The level scene runs the sim at 35 Hz (`TICRATE`) with one `ticcmd` per tic, independent of the frame rate (same checksums from 24 to 240 fps, T4.7/T4.9); the player moves with the twin-stick controls (keyboard and mouse, gamepad checked by script) and vanilla's movement and collision (blockmap, 24-unit step-up, dropoffs, sliding), matching vanilla tic for tic on every recorded route (T4.8, T4.8a); positions and angles are interpolated between tics without jitter; a tic takes under 0.1 ms on E1M9 with all things spawned (SPEC §9: 2 ms). The optional hands-on walk at real refresh rates (T4.9a) was done by the user on 2026-10-07: no jitter.

Decide where the shared fixed-point code lives: `IsoDoom.Map` already has `m_fixed` (`Fixed.cs`) and `m_bbox`; the sim references the map library or both move to a shared library. Log the choice in SPEC §12. SPEC §6.1.

No tic runs without a player mobj. The placeholder's own walking and facing are gone. **Interpolation (decision):** as source ports (Crispy Doom's `oldx`/`interp`), `mobj_t` has presentation-only `oldx`/`oldy`/`oldz`/`oldangle` and `interp`; `World.P_StoreInterpolation` (called first in `P_Ticker`) copies every mobj's position and sets `interp`, a mobj spawned during a tic has it false (so is drawn where it is), T5.6's teleport must clear it; none of it is in the checksum. `LevelScene.Interpolated` draws a mobj at `TicFraction` (the banked time in tics) between the two, the angle the short way. **Drawing:** `PresentWorld` sets every frame the player's billboard (`PlayerPlaceholder` renamed `src/Render/PlayerSprite.cs`, now set from the player mobj: interpolated position and angle, `subsector->sector`, `state` sprite/frame, `FF_FULLBRIGHT`) and `Things` from the world's mobjs but the player's in thinker order (`DrawnMobjs`; rebuilt when mobjs come or go; voodoo dolls drawn, as vanilla), through `ThingEntry(mobj_t, …)` (radius for the wall pull, actor flag, shadow). T3.6's note: `ThingSprites.Entry.FloorZ` puts the blob shadow on the mobj's `floorz` (shadows stay off by default: look at them with flyers in M6, SPEC §13 Q3). The game camera follows the drawn player (`IsoCamera.Follow`, `Snap` on map loads and `place`), the light origin and the cutaway centre are its position and feet. The overlay shows the player mobj (state), the tic, checksum and last `ticcmd`. **Scripting** (`src/Game/LevelScript.cs`): `cmd FORWARD SIDE ANGLETURN [BUTTONS] [TICS]` queues raw commands, `step TICS` queues tics built from the input and waits; with either the scene runs **scripted tics** (`LevelScene.ScriptedTics`: only queued tics, paced at 35 Hz, the world still when the queue is empty, from the first frame when the script has one), so results do not depend on the frame rate; `checksum` (leveltime, `World.Checksum()`, the player mobj), `tics N`, `sim live|scripted`, `smooth FRAMES` (the drawn player's step per frame). `place` moves the player mobj (`World.PlaceMobj`, not vanilla: a debug move to the floor, no momentum, `S_PLAY`, not interpolated); `visible`/`walkto`/`tour` measure the player's billboard and pause the sim while capturing (monsters' frames would otherwise count as visible pixels). **Checked:** `--fixed-fps 30`, `60` and `144` on DOOM1 E1M1, headless and under lavapipe: `cmd 50 0 16384 0 35; cmd 0 50 0 0 20; cmd 35 35 8192 0 40; cmd 0 0 -16384 0 10; checksum; down W; step 35; up W; down D; down Shift; step 30; up D; up Shift; checksum` prints `975073776b24a10e` (leveltime 105) and `1c7191a048b64073` (170) at all three; running north (`cmd 50 0 16384 0 50; tics 15; smooth …`) gives the same checksum at all three, and per-frame steps change by at most 0.24 units at 144 fps (3.1–4.1 units a frame while accelerating), 0.52 at 60 and 1.30 at 30 (a frame spans 1.17 tics), i.e. no stalls or jumps; screenshots show the running `PLAY` frames, the camera and the cutaway following; live keyboard input (`hold W`) walks the player, not under the overview; `walkto`, `place`, `visible` and `tour 256` on E1M1 run. Keyboard walking by hand and judging the smoothness on a real display are for T4.9. **Level check:** the billboards are compared with the world's mobjs (each entry is its mobj's) and with T3.2's spawn lists (voodoo dolls aside); new game-loop check on every map: 4 tics of walking, then the billboards follow the mobjs at tic fractions 0, ½ and 1 (the player at the midpoint), a barrel spawned between tics is drawn where it is and dropped when removed. `InterpolationTests` (5): new mobjs not interpolated, each tic stores the start position, a mobj spawned mid-tic, `PlaceMobj`, interpolation state outside the checksum. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (752), `--level-check` headless (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic) and under lavapipe (DOOM1, synthetic), the Linux export, its smoke test and its level check (headless, synthetic) pass. See SPEC §12.
  *Note (from T4.7):* the camera lags a running player by about 50–75 units (T3.3's smoothing at 10/s plus the look-ahead towards a cursor left at the screen centre): judge it while walking by hand in T4.9. Live tics also run under the overview and free-fly cameras (the player stands); the sim does not pause when the window loses focus (menus and pause: M7). A cursor aim makes scripted runs depend on the frame rate (the camera, so the cursor ground point, follows with smoothing); scripts that compare checksums aim with `cmd`'s angle or not at all.
  *Note (from T3.3):* replace `LevelScene.UpdateGameCamera`'s placeholder movement with the player mobj; keep `IsoCamera.Follow(target, cursorGround, delta)` (and `Snap` on teleports and map loads).
  *Note (from T3.5):* things are drawn from `LevelScene.Things` (`ThingSprites.SetEntries`/`SetEntry`: map position, BAM angle, sector, sprite, frame, full bright) built from `SpawnedThings`; feed them from the mobjs instead (interpolated position, `mobj->angle`, `subsector->sector`, `state->sprite`/`frame`). The atlas already holds every sprite frame of the WAD. The player's sprite is `PlayerPlaceholder`'s `ThingSprites`.
  *Note (from T3.5a):* billboard entries carry the thing's radius (`ThingSprites.Entry.Radius`, the wall pull); entries built from mobjs must set it from `mobj->radius`.
  *Note (from T4.5):* run each tic with `World.G_Ticker(cmd)` (or the per-player array), not `P_Ticker`, so the command lands in `player_t.cmd`. `player_t.viewz` (eye height, bob included) is computed every tic if the presentation wants a bobbing camera or light origin; interpolate it like positions.
  *Note (from T4.2):* the `World` changes its `Level`'s sectors in place (SPEC §12 T4.2), so the level scene must hand the world the same `Level` it builds the mesh from (one `Level.Load` per world), and must not reuse a `Level` across worlds or reloads. Mobjs are `World.Mobjs()` in thinker order (`mobj_t.subsector.sector`, `state`, `sprite`, `frame`, `radius`); the player is `players[consoleplayer].mo`. `World.Checksum()` is the checksum to print.
  *Note (from T4.6):* each tic, call `LevelScene.BuildTiccmd` (or `TiccmdBuilder.G_BuildTiccmd` with `GameInput.Take`) once with the player mobj's `x`/`y`/`angle` instead of the placeholder's, and feed the result to `World.G_Ticker`; call `GameInput.Poll()` every frame (it latches taps between tics; it now runs in `UpdateGameCamera`), and `TiccmdBuilder.Reset()` on a map load (`LoadMap` does). Drop the placeholder's own walking (`UpdateGameCamera`) and facing then. Only build commands while the game camera is current (the free-fly camera shares W/A/S/D, E and Space).

## M5 — Map specials

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* doors, lifts, switches, teleporters, light specials, damage floors and exits work; keys too; E1M1–E1M9 can be completed without monsters (`-nomonsters`).

*Order:* T5.1 → T5.2 → T5.3–T5.8 (any order; T5.6 needs T5.2) → T5.9 → T5.10. Each task adds its cases to a synthetic specials map in `tools/SyntheticIwad` (a new map, e.g. `E1M2`, so `E1M1` stays stable for the existing checks) and tests them in CI.

**M5 is done (T5.10, 2026-10-07): its exit criterion (SPEC §11) is met.** Doors, lifts, switches, teleporters, light specials, damage floors, exits and keys are ported from vanilla and tested on the synthetic specials map in CI (T5.1–T5.8). Every E1 map has a `-nomonsters` route from its start to its exit that matches vanilla on every tic (T5.9; E1M8's exit needs the barons' death, so its route starts in the arena). The same routes reach their exits in the level scene, also as one game through the whole episode (T5.10). A tic takes under 0.03 ms (p95) on the busiest map (SPEC §9: 2 ms). Optional M5 task still open, not needed for the criterion: T5.1a (render check on the synthetic E1M2). The hand play (T5.10a) was done by the user on 2026-10-07: nothing jerky.

- [x] **T5.1 Sector data from the sim.** Feed the sim's sector floor, ceiling and light values to `LevelMesh`'s data texture every tic, interpolated between tics for heights (lights step, as in vanilla), and per-side texture changes (switches, T5.4) through a per-side or per-section texture slot that can change at run time instead of a fixed vertex attribute. Decide how a changed texture that is not in the level's atlas is handled (switch textures are known at load from `P_InitSwitchList`; add them to the atlas up front) and log it. Port p_spec.c's lookup helpers (`getSide`, `getSector`, `twoSided`, `getNextSector`, `P_FindLowestFloorSurrounding`, `P_FindHighestFloorSurrounding`, `P_FindNextHighestFloor`, `P_FindLowestCeilingSurrounding`, `P_FindHighestCeilingSurrounding`, `P_FindSectorFromLineTag`, `P_FindMinSurroundingLight`) and `P_ChangeSector`/`PIT_ChangeSector` (p_map.c; things that no longer fit; crushing damage waits for M6's `P_DamageMobj`, so keep a hook).
  *Note (from T4.7):* the level scene runs the tics (`LevelScene.RunTics`/`Tic`) and draws at `TicFraction` (`PresentWorld`); sector heights need old values per sector as mobjs have (`P_StoreInterpolation` in `P_Ticker`, presentation-only and outside the checksum), then `LevelMesh.UpdateSectors` with the interpolated heights each frame. The world already changes the mesh's own `Level` sectors in place.
  *Done when:* moving a sector in the sim moves it on screen smoothly, `--level-check` checks a run-time texture change, and the helpers are unit-tested on the synthetic map.
  *Note (from T4.2):* the sim writes heights and light straight into the map's `Sector` objects (`sector_t` forwards to them), so the data texture can read `Level.Sectors` after each tic; keep the previous tic's heights for the interpolation.
  *Done:* **Heights:** `P_StoreInterpolation` also stores each sector's floor and ceiling (`sector_t.oldfloorheight`/`oldceilingheight`, outside the checksum); `LevelScene.PresentWorld` writes them interpolated at `TicFraction` every frame through `LevelMesh.UpdateSectors(heights)` (`InterpolatedHeights`), with the current light and floor flat; only changed texels are uploaded. **Run-time textures:** wall quads refer to their sidedef texture (`CUSTOM0.y` = 3 × side + part) whose slot is in the new `side_textures` data texture (-1 = none, the quad collapses); floors take their flat slot from the sector data's A; the sim changes `Side`/`Sector` names and `UpdateSectors` re-points the texels; a pegged texture bottom anchors on the slot's height in the shader (`WallSection.BottomPegged`, `TextureTopFor`). Every texture of a switch pair the level draws is in the atlas up front (`Sim/Switches.cs`: p_switch.c `alphSwitchList` per game mode; `LevelMesh.Build(textureGroups:)`, `LevelScene.SwitchGroups`); a name the atlas lacks keeps the old texture (`RuntimeMisses`, a warning). **Sim:** `side_t` and `World.sides`, `sector_t.lines`/`linecount`/`blockbox`, p_spec.c's helpers in `World.Spec.cs` (`P_FindNextHighestFloor` with Chocolate Doom's overrun emulation), p_map.c `P_ChangeSector`/`PIT_ChangeSector`/`P_ThingHeightClip` (`crushchange`, `nofit`; the damage goes to the `P_DamageMobj` stub). **Synthetic specials map:** the synthetic IWAD's `E1M2` (a corridor with 24 alcoves, tags, lights, the `SW1BRCOM`/`SW2BRCOM` pair; `SpecTests` test every helper, `P_ChangeSector`'s cases and the interpolation store on it). **Level check:** on every map, a sector moved in the sim during a tic is drawn at its interpolated heights (fractions 0, ½, 1) with its things kept on the floor, and a wall texture (its switch partner where it has one) and a floor flat are changed at run time and back (texels, GPU upload, chunk attributes evaluated with the new texture's height); with a renderer the changed wall is compared side-on (DOOM1 E1M1: SW1STRTN → SW2STRTN, 6820 pixels; synthetic E1M1: PANEL → BRICK1 on a pegged upper, 3500 pixels). **Debug:** the level script's `plane SECTOR floor|ceiling HEIGHT [SPEED]` and `smooth FRAMES SECTOR` (at 60 fps a 2-unit/tic plane steps exactly 1.17 units every frame). Decisions in SPEC §12 (T5.1).

## M6 — Monsters & weapons

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* info.c states, AI, all Doom 1 shareware weapons and monsters, pickups, damage, aim assist; the status bar HUD. The shareware IWAD has zombiemen (`POSS`), shotgun guys (`SPOS`), imps (`TROO`), demons and spectres (`SARG`) and barons (`BOSS`), and the fist, chainsaw, pistol, shotgun, chaingun and rocket launcher; cacodemons, lost souls, the Cyberdemon, the Spider Mastermind, the plasma rifle and the BFG are registered-only (M9).

*Order:* T6.1 → T6.2 and T6.3 → T6.4 → T6.5 → T6.6 → T6.7–T6.11 (any order) → T6.12 → T6.13. T6.10 can come first if sound events are needed earlier.

They cover real combat against E1M3's, E1M5's and E1M7's monsters (zombiemen, shotgun guys, imps, demons and spectres; no barons; 61 kills in `DEMO1`), the pistol, shotgun and rocket launcher (the chaingun and chainsaw are in the `testmap-weapons` route, T6.6), pickups, keys, doors, lifts and secrets, and all three end with the player's death (`P_DeathThink`, the dead face, the lowering weapon: the T6.4, T6.6 and T6.11 notes). A corrupted dump line fails the test at that tic (checked). The level script's new `demo LUMP [TICS]` plays a demo in the level scene; there all three end with the player where vanilla's is (same `x`, `y`, angle, death frame); screenshots of `DEMO1` under lavapipe (combat, rockets, corpses, pickups and their messages, the status bar's face, keys and ammo, the damage palette) look right. **Tic budget (SPEC §9; the T4.9 and T6.4 notes):** `TicBudgetTests` gains an `all` case (every monster's target the player and its see state, as the new script command `wake` does): DOOM1 E1M9 skill 4, 87 monsters chasing: 0.11 ms median, 0.27 ms p95, 0.83 ms worst (Debug test host). In the game (`power invulnerability; wake; …; tictime 700`, 103 monsters hunting the player, who fires): 0.32–0.37 ms median, 0.63–0.67 ms p95 (editor, Debug), 0.13 ms median, 0.27–0.29 ms p95 (release export); a deterministic queued run (`--fixed-fps 35`, `cmd` tics) has its worst tic at the same tic every run, 0.9–1.0 ms. Live runs show single tics of 2.1–3.8 ms at a different tic each run, with 4–6 garbage collections in 700 tics (`tictime` now names the worst tic and counts the collections): frame and GC jitter, not a heavy tic; left to T8.3 (note there). **Every E1 map at every skill** (45 headless runs: the gamepad's stick walking and turning, RT firing, 1500 frames live, then `tictime 350`): no crash, error or unported call; the player is hurt, killed (E1M2, E1M5, E1M6, E1M9 on the hard skills) and kills; tic time 0.05–0.36 ms median, at most 0.62 ms p95. **Notes:** the T5.9 note is covered by `e1m8-barons` since T6.4 (the barons killed by route events, `A_BossDeath` lowering sector 30, in sync with vanilla); the T6.4 and T6.12 notes' death routes are `testmap-death`, `e1m8-death` (T6.12) and now the three demos; the in-game tic measure (T4.9, T6.4 notes) is above; the notes that need a person (T6.5's missiles behind walls, T6.6's muzzle flash and weapon cycling, T6.7's aim assist tuning, T6.11's HUD, T6.12's death and reborn in play) are T6.13a's checklist. The aim assist itself is checked in the sim by `AimAssistTests` (T6.7); a scripted in-game comparison at close range showed nothing either way (a zombieman 200 units away is about ±6° wide, and the pistol's refire spread hides the cone), so tuning stays a play question. `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1292, none skipped with both WADs and the dumps; all vanilla routes regenerated and unchanged), `--level-check` headless (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic) and under lavapipe (DOOM1, synthetic), the viewer check headless and under lavapipe (DOOM1, synthetic), and the export with its smoke test pass. - [x] **T6.13l Fog of war (user request).** Under the iso camera the player sees the whole map from the start, and monsters through walls, which vanilla never shows (the T3.8 note on the cursor cutaway is one case). Hide what the player has not seen. The user decided: **line of sight** decides what is seen (looking into a room through a window or an open door counts, not only walking in); **undiscovered sectors are drawn dimmed** by default (the layout shows, the detail does not), with a fully hidden style as an option; and it is a **fog of war**: a discovered sector the player cannot see right now keeps its geometry drawn normally, but its monsters are hidden until they are in sight again. Presentation only: `Sim/` and its checksums do not change, and with the option off everything draws as now (SPEC §6.3's spirit for a presentation deviation).
  - **Three states per sector:** *unseen* (never in sight), *discovered* (seen at some time this level) and *visible* (in sight now). Compute the visible set from the player mobj's position (the sim's, after each tic; a dead player keeps its last set) with a **360° walk of the BSP** as r_bsp.c's `R_RenderBSPNode`/`R_AddLine`/`R_ClipSolidWallSegment` do, over the full circle instead of the view's 90°: a one-sided line, or a two-sided one closed as `R_AddLine`'s closed-door test (back ceiling ≤ front floor or back floor ≥ front ceiling), blocks; anything else (windows, steps, open doors) lets sight through. `Level` already has the nodes, segs and subsectors; `World.Sight.cs` (`P_CheckSight`, REJECT) is the other tool if per-thing checks turn out to be needed. A sector is visible when one of its subsectors is reached with an unclipped span. Decide whether a subsector reached only through a very thin sliver counts. Keep it cheap: measure it on Doom II's biggest maps (MAP18, MAP27) and keep it well under SPEC §9's frame budget (a target of 0.2 ms; it may run every tic, not every frame).
  - **`ML_MAPPED`:** the same walk sets vanilla's seen-line flag (`Line.ML_MAPPED`, src/Map/MapTypes.cs; nothing sets it yet) on each seg's line it draws an unclipped span of, as r_segs.c does when vanilla's renderer draws a line. T7.9's automap needs exactly this (SPEC §7.6). Vanilla's renderer sets it on the game's `line_t` and `P_ArchiveWorld` saves line flags (`World.SaveG.cs` already writes `li.flags`), and `World.Checksum` leaves line flags out. So decide whether the walk writes it into the sim's `line_t.flags` between tics (as vanilla's renderer does; saves keep it for free) or keeps its own copy, and log the choice in SPEC §12. Either way the discovered set must survive a save and load (T7.6): derive it from the mapped lines at load (a sector is discovered when one of its lines is mapped), or save it in `SaveGame.cs`'s part. It resets with each level load and each reborn's reload, as vanilla's `ML_MAPPED` does. With `pw_allmap` (the computer map) vanilla's automap shows the unmapped lines in grey: decide whether the computer map also discovers the layout here (it would show dimmed, not lit), and log it.
  - **Drawing:** a new per-sector data texture (`sector_data`'s four channels are taken; e.g. `sector_fog`, R8, the same layout, the fog state per sector, uploaded only when it changes, as `UpdateSectors` does) read by the level shader for every kind (floors, walls, masked middles and their backs, cutaway caps, lids, wall caps, island walls). A wall shows its own sector's state; decide the two-sided cases (a lower or upper wall between a discovered and an unseen sector) so a doorway into an unseen room does not leave a lit sheet hanging, and log it. **Dim** (the default): unseen surfaces drawn in the palette, darker and greyer. For example a fixed dark colormap row with the light diminishing ignored, mapped to its nearest grey (a 256-entry palette lookup built on the CPU, as `Fuzz.NearestIndex` builds its), or the cutaway's 4×4 dither over the void. Try two or three, take screenshots under the game camera (DOOM1 E1M1 from the start; E1M2's outdoor area; Doom II MAP01) and **let the user pick** before finishing: the user turned down earlier changes on their looks. **Hide:** unseen surfaces are not drawn at all (the void's colour shows). A sector that becomes discovered may fade in over a few frames (optional, presentation time only).
  - **Things:** the player's own sprite is always drawn. Anything that acts or moves (monsters, missiles, barrels, every `MF_SHOOTABLE` or `MF_MISSILE` thing, corpses that may be raised later) is drawn only while its sector is *visible*. Static things (items, keys, decorations) are drawn once their sector is *discovered*. Unseen sectors' things are never drawn. Blob shadows, the fuzz pass and teleport fog follow their thing. Shot tracers (T6.13j) still show the player's own shots, and with `all` a monster's only from a visible shooter. Sounds are unaffected (an unseen monster is still heard, as vanilla). The aim assist (T6.7, sim) is not affected. Check that the cursor cutaway (`--level-cutaway-cursor`) no longer reveals monsters in closed rooms, and settle the T3.8 note above.
  - **Option:** `--level-fog=dim|hide|off` (default `dim`), `gameplay/fog` in `Settings.cs` (saved as the others; T7.3's Gameplay page gets an item), a debug key that cycles it (a free one, e.g. N; F1's list and `CLAUDE.md`'s key list updated). It applies under the game camera. The overview and free-fly debug cameras draw everything, unless that turns out to be confusing. The overlay (F3) gains a line: the style, the visible, discovered and total sector counts, and the walk's cost. Level script: `fog` prints the same, and `seen SECTOR unseen|discovered|visible` fails unless the sector is in that state, so a check can walk the player with `walkto` or `place` and assert it.
  - **CPU reference and checks:** the walk is pure C# with no Godot types (e.g. `src/Render/FogOfWar.cs`, or in `src/Map` if it is to be shared with T7.9), unit tested on the synthetic IWAD (`tools/SyntheticIwad`'s E1M1 and E1M2: a room behind a closed door stays unseen and opens up when the door opens, a window lets sight through, a one-sided wall blocks, the 360° includes behind the player, `ML_MAPPED` set on the right lines) and, where present, on DOOM1 E1M1 (the start room's neighbours). `--level-check`: on every map the player's start sector is visible on the first tic, a sector known to be out of sight is unseen, a door sector's far side becomes discovered once the door opens in the sim, and a save or load keeps the discovered set. With a real renderer, an unseen floor's pixels are compared with the CPU's dim (or hide) mapping, and a monster in a discovered but out-of-sight room is not drawn while one in sight is. Log the decisions in SPEC §12 (T6.13l), and mention the option in SPEC §7 where the cutaway and lids are.

then as `G_InitNew`: unpaused, `M_ClearRandom`, a user game, its skill, `G_DoLoadLevel`'s forced wipe, so a load melts, from the title or over a level, and `IGameHost.G_LoadGame` shows the map with the save's world), `SaveDir` (per IWAD: `user://savegames/doom1.wad/isodoomsavN.dsg`), `SaveDescription` (the menus' slots, through `IMenuHost.SaveDescription`; any version's, so an old save shows and says why it won't load). m_menu.c's quicksave and quickload (`M_QuickSave`, `M_QuickLoad`, their responses, `quickSaveSlot`, `QSPROMPT`, `QLPROMPT`, `QSAVESPOT`, `QLOADNET`, `key_menu_qsave`/`qload`), the input actions `quicksave` (F6) and `quickload` (F9) on the Actions page (`project.godot`, `GameInput.Actions`, `Settings.ControlPages`, read as menu keys by `LevelScene.MenuActionKey`). **Not vanilla:** a pad's A (or a left click) on an empty save slot fills in the map and its time (`E1M3 2:05`, `MMenu.DefaultSaveDescription`), which A saves. **The scene** (`LevelScene.SaveGame.cs`): the save directory (`--savedir=DIR`; headless runs, the checks, scripts and screenshots save to a temporary directory of their own, removed at the end), the trial load and the load (the mesh built from a fresh copy of the map, then the save's world on another; the save's tweaks, but the aim assist's cone the option's when the aiming tweak matches). Level script: `save SLOT [DESCRIPTION]`, `load SLOT [refused]`. **Checked:** `SaveGameTests` (saved at a route's start, a quarter, half, three quarters and its last tics, loaded into a new world, saved again to the same bytes, and played on with every tic's checksum and every second's whole state the same as the run never saved: every DOOM1 exit route, so E1M1–E1M9 and E1M3's secret exit, the monster fights, the deaths and reborns, DOOM1's three demos, and every synthetic and test map route, in CI too; a removed mobj kept as a missile's target; cut and damaged data refused) and `SaveLoadTests` (the menus' save with the next tic and load going on as the saved game; from the title, melting; during a level, melting; no save without a game on a level or a directory; no save, older, newer, damaged, cut short, empty slot, another game mode, a map the WAD lacks, the state's map not the header's: refused with their messages, the game kept; quicksave picking a slot then asking, quickload; the pad's default description); `--level-check` (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic; headless, and DOOM1 under lavapipe): F6 picks slot 3, a typed description, the save with the next tic, F9 and Y load it back, 70 tics on the same checksums, a damaged save refused; a script on DOOM1 (`route …e1m1-exit.route 300; tics 300; save 0 test; checksum; cmd 25 0 300 0 50; checksum; load 0; checksum; cmd 25 0 300 0 50; checksum`: the same checksums twice). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (1470, none skipped with both WADs) pass. ## M8 — Shareware complete

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* all of E1 is playable from start to finish on desktop Linux and the Steam Deck; polish pass.

- [ ] **T8.1 Steam Deck.** Build and run on a Deck (native Linux build): gamepad layout and Steam Input defaults, UI and HUD scale at 1280×800, frame cap options (60/40/30, SPEC §9), suspend and resume, the IWAD search on the SD card (T1.1c). Measure frame time on all E1 maps.
  *Done when:* E1M1–E1M9 run at a locked 60 fps on the Deck, or follow-up tasks say what is short. Needs a person with a Deck.
  *Note (from T6.11):* the HUD's automatic scale at 1280×800 is 4 (the bar 128 pixels tall, the message font's capitals 28): judge it on the Deck's 7" screen (`--level-hud-scale`).
  *Note (from T7.8a):* time the OPL3 chip on the Deck: `dotnet test -c Release --filter-method IsoDoom.Tests.Audio.Opl3Tests.OneMinuteOfOpl3Output` prints its share of a core (worst case; 2.9–3.6% on the desktop, about 4–5% estimated for the Deck, SPEC §12 T7.8a). If the music thread's cost shows, port the rest of Nuked-OPL3-fast (Chocolate Doom's `opl/opl3.c`, bit-exact, about twice as fast as upstream; `tools/OplRef/check.sh` and `Opl3Tests` keep it exact).
  *Note (from T7.8e):* the Debug build (the editor's `godot`, F5) renders the OPL with an unoptimized JIT at 26–50% of a core (74% under lavapipe's load): no underrun here, but on a slower machine a debug run may drop out where the export does not; if it does, build the audio code in its own always-optimized assembly (`<Optimize>true</Optimize>`; `MethodImplOptions.AggressiveOptimization` does nothing in a debuggable assembly, tried) — T8.1 measures the export on the Deck (`underruns` and the overlay's `% now`).
  *Note (from T7.8g):* the music thread's overlay cost (`% now`) is wall time just after a 2 ms sleep, so it reads 3–4× the render's work on a clocked-down core (7.8% against 2.1% busy, SPEC §12 T7.8g); judge the Deck by `underruns`, not by it. If T7.8f or this task hears dropouts, add the ring buffer's size (`--level-music-buffer`) to More Options → Sound (T7.8g left it out).
- [ ] **T8.2 Gamepad aiming.** SPEC §13 Q5: right-stick aiming loses cursor distance. Tune aim assist strength on a pad, and try a lock-on toggle and a virtual cursor at a fixed or stick-scaled distance (for the camera's look-ahead). Log the result and close Q5.
  *Note (from T6.7):* the aim assist's strength is `Tweaks.AimAssistCone` (SPEC §12 T6.7), a sim setting every player of a game shares, like the skill; a stronger cone for a pad player only would have to move it into `player_t` (set at the game's start, part of the lockstep settings) or into the `ticcmd`. A lock-on can choose the absolute angle in `TiccmdBuilder` without touching the sim.
  *Done when:* E1 can be played comfortably with a pad (by a person) and the decision is logged.
- [x] **T8.3 Performance pass.** Check every SPEC §9 target: 60+ fps at 1080p on integrated graphics for all E1 maps (with T2.9a's measurements), sim under 2 ms per tic on E1M9 with every monster awake, load under 1 s. Profile and fix what falls short (draw calls, overdraw, shader lookups, allocations per tic).
  *Done when:* the numbers are in SPEC §12 and meet §9, or have follow-up tasks.
  *Note (from T6.13):* the sim's tic time on E1M9 with every monster awake is far under the budget (0.13 ms median, 0.29 ms p95 in the release export; a deterministic run's worst tic 1.0 ms), but live runs show single tics of 2.1–3.8 ms at a different tic each run, with 4–6 garbage collections in 700 tics (`tictime` counts them): cut the allocations per tic and per frame (sim events, lists in the presentation), and check the spikes again in a release build on the Deck.
  *Note (from T4.9):* the first tics of a map took up to 6.6 ms in the game (JIT compiling the sim's paths; later tics under 0.2 ms on E1M9). Within a 60 fps frame, but on top of the first frames' rendering: look at it with `tictime` (SPEC §12 T4.9) on the Deck, and consider ReadyToRun or warming the sim up during the load if it shows as a hitch.
  *Note (from T6.9):* the fuzz pass (`shaders/sprite.gdshaderinc`, `FUZZ`) matches each fuzzed pixel's background to the palette by a 256-entry search; if it shows in a profile (many spectres up close), replace it with an inverse-palette lookup texture (RGB → index, one per palette, rebuilt with `SetPalette`).
  *Progress (2026-10-15, an agent's session: no GPU person, no Deck; not done):* **Allocations and the JIT, the parts that need no person** (SPEC §12 T8.3). **Found:** the 4–6 collections of T6.13's note (and 150 in 700 tics in the editor's Debug build) were not the sim's: the sim allocates only the mobjs it spawns (puffs, blood, missiles). In a Debug build every read of a span over static multi-byte data (`finesine`, `finetangent`, `tantoangle`, `Ticcmds.angleturn`, the OPL3's `logsinrom`/`exprom`) allocated (`RuntimeHelpers.CreateSpan`'s field stub), about 700 KB a frame from the music thread alone: now arrays behind the same span properties (the OPL3's cost unchanged, 5.0–5.5% of a core in Release). The rest was the presentation's per-frame garbage: the F3 overlay's text (now ten times a second), `OS.GetCmdlineUserArgs` marshalled at each `HasUserArg` (now read once), a `StringName` made at each `Input` call and shader parameter (`GameInput`'s actions and `StringNames` made once), `CursorGround.Pick`'s sorted set, closures and interface enumerators in `LevelMesh`, `WallCaps`, `NearActors`, the thing billboards' arrays at each change of the drawn set (lists refilled), `World.Mobjs()`'s iterator (a struct enumerator), the settings' sync formatting every setting every frame (now four times a second and at exit; the volumes and mouse sensitivity still every frame) and the sound lump names. **Measured** (headless, E1M9 skill 4, every monster hunting the invulnerable player, who fires; `tictime` now prints the bytes allocated a tic and a frame): release export, 700 live tics: 4 → **0** collections, worst tic 2.0–3.2 → 0.7–1.0 ms, ~390 bytes a frame (the level script's own awaits); editor (Debug, overlay shown): 154 → 1 collection, ~760 KB → 1.7 KB a frame; overlay hidden, 2500 tics: 0 collections, 16 bytes a tic. `TicAllocationTests` (synthetic E1M1 in CI, DOOM1 E1M9, Doom II MAP29) keeps a tic under 192 bytes (36, 69, 106 now; 253 and 471 before in Debug). **JIT warm-up:** a background thread compiles every non-generic method of Sim, Map and Wad and of the per-tic presentation types at start (`JitWarmup`, 2109 methods in 190–290 ms, `--no-jit-warmup` to measure); nothing runs, so the checksums cannot change (DEMO1's checksum at tic 2000 is the same with, without and before it). The first 105 tics with monsters woken (the spike is tic 19, the first attacks): export worst 4.9–8.0 → 0.7–1.1 ms, editor 7.6–11.0 → 0.8–2.0 ms. Loads unchanged (`--level-check`: Doom II's slowest, MAP01, 544 ms against 635 ms before on this busy machine; DOOM1 240 ms, under lavapipe 304 ms). ReadyToRun was tried (Godot's export honours `PublishReadyToRun` for all three exports from Linux): loads 158 → 100 ms on E1M9, but the export crashed in 2 of 4 Doom II level checks, so not adopted (**T8.3a**). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test`, `dotnet format`, `--level-check` headless (DOOM1, Doom II, synthetic; the export too) and under lavapipe (DOOM1), and the viewer check pass. **Left for people:** the fps at 1080p on integrated graphics (T2.9a's measurements) and the Deck's tic spikes and fps in a release build (T8.1); then tick this task.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* the fps at 1080p on integrated graphics is T2.9a's: every E1 map above 180 fps on Intel UHD Graphics (Tiger Lake-H GT1; 250 in the overview), MAP29 230; the collections stay flat in play. With the tic budget, the allocations and the loads above, every §9 target but the Deck's is met; the Deck's fps, tic spikes and JIT warm-up move to T8.1 (note there). SPEC §12 T8.3.
- [x] **T8.3a ReadyToRun exports** (found in T8.3). `<PublishReadyToRun>true</PublishReadyToRun>` for the `ExportRelease` configuration in `IsoDoom.csproj` precompiles the assemblies in all three exports from Linux (DLLs about 2.8 times larger: `IsoDoom.dll` 1.45 → 4.1 MB), loads E1M9 in 100 ms instead of 158 and leaves the JIT warm-up 21 ms of work, but the Linux export segfaulted in 2 of 4 headless Doom II level checks (in the automap section, `T7.9`), the JIT export in none of 4. Find the crash (a core dump, Godot's and .NET's versions), then adopt it if it is ours or fixed upstream.
  *Done when:* the exports are ReadyToRun and CI's checks pass on all three, or the reason not to is logged in SPEC §12.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* `PublishReadyToRun` for `ExportRelease` in `IsoDoom.csproj`: all three exports are precompiled (`IsoDoom.dll` 1.47 → 4.1 MB; macOS per architecture). **The crash was not in the automap:** the release export's stdout is block-buffered, so the createdump line landed mid-log; it came after `Level check: OK`, at exit. The crash reports (createdump, once given the exec bit the export drops: T8.3c) show .NET's finalizer thread in `GodotObject.Finalize()` (a `Godot.Image`, 3410 objects queued: meshes, nodes, variants) calling into the engine while its main thread is already past `main()`, tearing down: Godot's .NET shutdown leaves the finalizer thread running. Rare in both builds, more often with ReadyToRun's timing (3 in 66 Doom II checks, 0 in 36 for the JIT export). Ours to avoid: `Main._ExitTree` (the root's, after the music thread stops) runs the finalizers while the engine is whole and holds the GC off (a no-GC region) for the teardown; 0 in 80 since. Measured (DOOM1, headless, quiet machine): the slowest load E1M1 255 → 140 ms, the WAD's opening 78 → 32 ms, the JIT warm-up 196 → 20 ms; the first 105 tics on E1M9 with monsters awake have no spike even without the warm-up (worst 1.0–1.3 ms, against 4.8–9.8 ms JIT; the warm-up stays for the editor). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test`, `dotnet format`, the level checks (Doom II ×80, DOOM1, synthetic; editor and export; DOOM1 and synthetic under lavapipe) and the viewer check pass; the Windows and macOS exports were built here, their CI jobs run on the next push (SPEC §12 T8.3a).
- [x] **T8.3b `AddSurfaceFromArrays` flake** (found in T8.3a). Under heavy load (8–12 level checks at once) about 1 Doom II level check in 50, in either export, fails a map: `LevelMesh.BuildChunks` gets Godot's `Condition "p_arrays.size() != ARRAY_MAX"` for an array just resized to `ARRAY_MAX`, then `no chunk` for that sector and more wall quads than expected (MAP10, MAP12, MAP15, MAP16 seen). Something corrupts native arrays: look at threads touching Godot collections or variants (the music thread, the finalizer thread, the JIT warm-up), reproduce with a loop of checks, fix.
  *Done when:* the cause is found and fixed, or logged in SPEC §12 with 100 loaded runs clean.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* **the cause was ours:** Godot's generated binding passes a `Godot.Collections.Array` to native code as a borrowed pointer (`arrays.NativeValue`, no `GC.KeepAlive`; the IL of `ArrayMesh.AddSurfaceFromArrays` checked), so in a Release build the wrapper is unreachable once the pointer is read, and a GC during the call (the music thread and the JIT warm-up allocate; more GCs under load) let the finalizer thread destroy the array under `add_surface_from_arrays`: its size read as garbage, the surface refused, the sector left without a chunk. **Fixed:** the five mesh builders' arrays are `using` (disposed after the call, never finalized). Its sibling: `GD.Load<PackedScene>(…).Instantiate()` in `Main` (the receiver's wrapper, the scene's only reference) now keeps its wrapper (`Main.Instantiate`). `ThingSprites`, `ItemBeams` and `MonsterMarkers` hold their multimeshes (and `ThingSprites` its meshes) in fields instead of fetching them back from the nodes, which raced Godot's GC-handle swap with the finalizer (`Handle is not initialized`) and cost a native call per instance and frame. **Reproduced** with `ISODOOM_GC_STRESS=1` (new, debugging: a thread collecting every 2 ms): the export before the fix failed 6 runs in 6 at once (`ARRAY_MAX` at three of the builders, the scene's segfault); after it 70 stressed Doom II checks ran to their end (their only failures the 1 s load budget, missed under the stress). **Loaded** (100 Doom II level checks of the release export, 10 at a time, no stress): before, 3 `ARRAY_MAX` failures (MAP14, MAP21, MAP24: `no chunk`, then the switch textures' checks) and 2 heap-corruption aborts; after, 99 clean and 1 heap-corruption abort, a separate bug that predates this one (**T8.3d**). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test`, `dotnet format`, the level checks (Doom II, DOOM1 stressed, synthetic, editor and export) and the viewer check pass (SPEC §12 T8.3b).
- [x] **T8.3c createdump in the exports** (found in T8.3a). Godot's Linux and macOS exports copy .NET's `createdump` without its exec bit (`-rw-r--r--`; the runtime pack's is `-rwxr--r--`), so `DOTNET_DbgEnableMiniDump=1` cannot write a dump (`Problem launching createdump ... Permission denied`). Restore it (a post-export step in CI and the docs, or upstream), or document `chmod +x` for debugging.
  *Done (2026-10-16):* **Linux only:** Godot copies each .NET file of the export as a shared object with `DirAccess::copy` and no mode (`editor_export_platform_pc.cpp`'s `export_project_data`), after the export plugins' `_export_end`, so no plugin of ours can restore it; the macOS export marks every Mach-O file 0755 (`is_executable`), its `createdump`s are `-rwxr-xr-x` in the zip; Windows needs no bit. **Restored after the export:** the documented export command (`AGENTS.md`) and CI's export step `chmod +x` `data_IsoDoom_linuxbsd_x86_64/createdump`, and the release job, whose downloaded artifacts lose every mode, gives it back with the game's, then checks both are executable in the Linux zip (checked locally on a zip made both ways). **Checked:** a fresh export killed with SIGSEGV under `DOTNET_DbgEnableMiniDump=1`: without the bit `Problem launching createdump ... Permission denied (13)`, with it a 317 MB minidump (`Dump successfully written`). An upstream fix (Godot keeping the source's mode) would make the step unneeded. `actionlint` passes (SPEC §12 T8.3c).
  *Done when:* a crash of an export writes a dump with `DOTNET_DbgEnableMiniDump=1`, or the workaround is documented.
- [x] **T8.3d Heap corruption under load** (found in T8.3b). About 1 Doom II level check in 50–100 of the release export, 10 at a time, aborts at a map load (`malloc_consolidate(): unaligned fastbin chunk detected`; MAP07, MAP10 seen) and then hangs instead of exiting (the runs stopped at a 20 min timeout); 2 in 100 before T8.3b's fixes, 1 in 100 after, and about 1 in 30 under `ISODOOM_GC_STRESS=1`, where Godot also logs `Handle is not initialized` in `ScriptManagerBridge.SwapGCHandleForType` from unrelated calls (`InputMap.ActionGetEvents`, `ResourceLoader.Load`, `GodotObject.Finalize`): the binding swaps a `RefCounted` wrapper's GC handle on the main thread while the finalizer thread frees it, a likely source. No dump yet (createdump wrote none for the abort, T8.3c). Get a native backtrace (a Godot template with symbols, or gdb attached to the hung process), find which object is freed twice, then fix it (dispose that wrapper on the main thread) or report it upstream with a reproducer.
  *Done when:* the cause is found and fixed or reported upstream, and 100 loaded runs are clean (or the rate is logged in SPEC §12).
  *Done (2026-10-16):* **the cause was ours again, T8.3b's class one level down:** Godot's binding passes a `Variant` argument as its bare native value, and a non-scalar `Variant` frees its data in a finalizer, so the mesh builders' `arrays[(int)Mesh.ArrayType.Vertex] = c.Vertices.ToArray()` could have the packed array freed by .NET's finalizer thread during `godotsharp_variant_new_copy` (its refcount bumped in a freed fastbin chunk); `AddSurfaceFromArrays`' null `blendShapes`/`lods` became the binding's own temporaries, freed the same way. **Found** with a Godot 4.7.2 release template built with symbols from the tag (headless: `scons platform=linuxbsd target=template_release module_mono_enabled=yes debug_symbols=yes x11=no wayland=no vulkan=no opengl3=no alsa=no pulseaudio=no udev=no dbus=no speechd=no fontconfig=no accesskit=no`, swapped in for the export's executable) under gdb: all 4 aborts in 120 stressed runs in `ArrayMesh::add_surface_from_arrays`, the finalizer thread in `PackedArrayRefBase::destroy` or `Dictionary::_unref`. **Fixed** (SPEC §12 T8.3d): `src/Render/GodotRefs.cs` (`SetSlot`, `AddSurface`, `ActionEvents`, `LoadShader`), `StringNames.SetParam` and `LevelMesh.SetParameter` dispose their `Variant`, the settings' and IWAD config's `ConfigFile`s and string `Variant`s are `using`, the level script's input events go through `Parse`, the level check holds its events (`Held`), labels' settings and inline materials are held across their setters, and the check's read-back `Image`s (its per-map `IndexedTextures.CreateImage(…).GetData()` among them) and the `SurfaceTool`s are kept alive to their scope's end (`GodotRefs.Keep`: disposing them was measured worse, below); the `InputMap.ActionGetEvents` arrays are disposed (the `Handle is not initialized` errors, upstream godotengine/godot#112067). `GodotLifetimeTests` (Cecil over the editor build's `IsoDoom.dll`) fails on such a temporary: 73 sites before, 0 after; AGENTS.md's invariants state the rule. **Measured**, Doom II level checks of the release export, 10 at a time: unstressed, 0 aborts or hangs in 100 (98 OK, 2 over the load budget with 10 checks on 8 cores: MAP01 in 1014 and 1141 ms); under `ISODOOM_GC_STRESS=1`, 1 in 300 (before: 7 aborts in 100, 3 `Handle is not initialized`), a different fault (T8.3f); the symbol build, 0 of 200, 8 at a time (before: 4 of 120). **A/B of the read-back `Image`s' and `SurfaceTool`s' `using`s** (an earlier round of this fix), stressed: with them, 7 in 450 (the GC faulting or spinning in `Ref_TraceNormalRoots`' strong-handle scan, after the cheats page), without them 0 in 450; the dummy renderer's `GetImage` returns the `Image` it holds, the same object each call, but 300,000 disposals of one under the stress, alone, stayed clean, so the mechanism is open; `GodotRefs.Keep` keeps them alive without disposing. Unstressed, the level builds are no slower (MAP17 49–73 ms against 60–107 ms). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test`, `dotnet format` and the level and viewer checks pass.
- [ ] **T8.3e Report the binding's lifetime bugs upstream** (found in T8.3d). Godot's C# binding keeps no argument alive across a native call: a `Variant` argument is passed as `(godot_variant)x.NativeVar`, a collection as its `NativeValue`, an object as `GetPtr(x)`, and for a null optional collection it makes `new Array<T>()`/`new Dictionary()` itself, so a GC during the call can finalize (free) what the call reads (T8.3b, T8.3d). Also, `_instance_binding_reference_callback` swaps a `RefCounted`'s GC handle without a lock, racing `godotsharp_internal_refcounted_disposed` on the finalizer thread (#112067). File an issue with a minimal reproducer (a C# script storing packed arrays into a `Godot.Collections.Array` and calling `AddSurfaceFromArrays` in a loop while another thread runs `GC.Collect(); GC.WaitForPendingFinalizers()`, on a release export), suggesting `GC.KeepAlive` on each argument after the call in the generated code. Needs a person to file it.
  *Done when:* the issue is filed (or an existing one updated) and linked here.
  *Prepared (2026-10-16), to file:* `tools/GodotLifetimeRepro/` is the reproducer (a standalone 4.7.2 project, one script: 100,000 `AddSurfaceFromArrays` with a thread collecting; `--case=` leaves one temporary unprotected) and `ISSUE.md` there the issue text, with the comment for the open godotengine/godot#112067 (the handle swap, `confirmed`; no new issue for it). No issue covers the arguments (searched; #124016 is the same bug for `this`, `Array.Count`). **Measured** on the official 4.7.2 template, 10 runs a case: the binding's own null `blendShapes`/`lods` crash every run (JIT and ReadyToRun), the `Array` argument every run, the plain code every run, and **without the collecting thread** too (the binding's `new Array<Array>()`, allocated after `arrays`' pointer was read, triggers the GC that frees it: 6 in 6); the `Variant` store alone never (0 in 20, nor 3 × 2,000,000 tight stores); all kept alive, 1 SIGSEGV in 110 JIT runs, cause open (#124016's?), 0 in 10 ReadyToRun. With a 4.7.2 template with symbols (rebuilt: T8.3d's `scons` line, after `apt install pkg-config` and SCons from PyPI), gdb: main thread in `RenderingServer::mesh_create_surface_data_from_arrays` under `add_surface_from_arrays`, the finalizer thread in `Dictionary::_unref` (the binding's `lods`). A loop of `InputMap.ActionGetEvents` alone did not reproduce #112067 (0 in 3 × 300,000). Left: a person files the issue with the project zipped (`git archive --format=zip -o GodotLifetimeRepro.zip HEAD:tools/GodotLifetimeRepro`), posts the comment, and links both here.

## M9 — Ultimate Doom

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* E2–E4, bosses and registered-only content. Needs a registered or Ultimate Doom IWAD (tests skip without it; add `ISODOOM_DOOM_WAD` as for Doom II).

- [x] **T9.1 Registered IWAD checks.** Do T1.4b and T1.5b (textures and sprites), then load every map of E1–E4 in `--level-check` within the load budget, and run the IWAD search with an Ultimate Doom install (T1.1c).
  *Done when:* the Doom 1 full-IWAD tests and `--level-check` pass on the IWAD.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* T1.4b and T1.5b done; with The Ultimate Doom's 2024 re-release (`wads/doom.wad`), `--level-check -iwad wads/doom.wad` loads and checks all 36 maps of E1–E4 and passes headless (slowest load E1M7, 327 ms; budget 1000 ms) and under Xvfb + lavapipe (slowest E1M1, 363 ms; about 2.5 min); `--viewer-check` passes too. One fix, in the check: the teleport check (T5.6) failed on E2M5 and E3M6, where monsters had teleported on earlier tics and their older fogs, interpolating, counted against "the fogs are not interpolated"; it now judges only the player's two fogs. 19 maps teleported the player. The missing-wall-texture counts in the load log (e.g. E4M5's 8) are the maps' own unset textures (DOOM1's E1M2 has its 2 too). DOOM1 and Doom II (`--level-check`) still pass. Also fixed: `SharewareDownload.IsVerified` compared a symbolic link's own length with the WAD's (a linked `wads/DOOM1.WAD` failed), now it follows the link (`ALinkToTheWadVerifies`). **Left:** the IWAD search with a real Ultimate Doom install stays T1.1c's (people, on real installs). SPEC §12 T9.1.
- [x] **T9.2 Registered monsters.** Cacodemon (`A_HeadAttack`), lost soul (`A_SkullAttack`, the charge in `P_XYMovement`/`PIT_CheckThing`), Cyberdemon (`A_CyberAttack`, `A_Hoof`, `A_Metal`) and Spider Mastermind (`A_SPosAttack` reuse, `A_SpidRefire`), with their bosses' `A_BossDeath` cases (E2M8, E3M8, E4M6, E4M8; vanilla v1.9 rules, including Ultimate Doom's).
  *Done when:* each monster and boss ending matches vanilla on a route.
  *Note (from T4.4):* `P_ZMovement` reverses a charging lost soul's `momz` on hitting the floor *before* the landing zeroes it, as linuxdoom-1.10 and Ultimate/Final Doom's executables; Doom II v1.9's (and registered Doom 1.9's) does it *after*, so the soul bounces. Chocolate Doom picks by `gameversion` (`correct_lost_soul_bounce`); decide how the sim picks (game mode, or a setting) and log it.
  *Note (from T6.4):* `A_BossDeath` is ported whole (`World.Enemy.cs`, with Chocolate Doom's `CheckBossEnd`: `GameMode.retail` counts as The Ultimate Doom) and its cases are tested on `TestMap`s (`MonsterAiTests.TheBossSpecials`); `P_SpawnMissile` and `World.MissileSpeed` (the fast `MT_HEADSHOT`) are in. Check the episodes' boss specials against vanilla routes with a registered or Ultimate IWAD.
  *Note (from T10.3):* the four monsters' actions (`A_HeadAttack`, `A_SkullAttack`, `A_CyberAttack`, `A_Hoof`, `A_Metal`, `A_SpidRefire`) are ported (`World.Enemy.Attacks.cs`) and match vanilla on Doom II routes (`map08-caco`, `map05-skull`, `map05-skull-floor`, `map08-cyber`, `map20-spider`); the lost soul's bounce is decided by game mode (SPEC §12 T10.3: only `retail` bounces before the landing; T10.7: by `gameversion` since, `ultimate` and the Final Doom executables bounce). Left here: the registered/Ultimate IWAD's routes, the bounce on a `retail` route, and the boss endings (E2M8, E3M8, E4M6, E4M8).
  *Note (groundwork, 2026-10-15):* routes take `iwad doom` (The Ultimate Doom's IWAD, `retail`, as `ultimate`; SPEC §12 M9) in the tests, the VanillaRef scripts and RouteGen (`doom-exmy-…`); `doom-e4m1-walk-exit`, `doom-e3m2-walk-exit` and its four demos (`demos.sh --doom`, E2M2's and E3M5's cacodemons and lost souls among them) match vanilla.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* routes on The Ultimate Doom's IWAD (`iwad doom`, `retail`, as `ultimate`; skill 1, monsters; RouteGen's `BossEnd`, T10.3's duel then the boss's death) match vanilla tic by tic, HUD included: `doom-e2m8-cyber` (the Cyberdemon's `A_Hoof`, `A_Metal`, `A_CyberAttack`; its `A_BossDeath` exits, the route's last tic), `doom-e3m8-spider` (`A_Metal`, `A_SPosAttack`, `A_SpidRefire`; exits), `doom-e4m6-cyber` (the doors tagged 666 open, blazeOpen) and `doom-e4m8-spider` (the floor tagged 666 lowers); `doom-e2m1-skull-floor` (`SkullFloor`, `map05-skull-floor`'s script) has a charging lost soul hit the floor and bounce before the landing, `retail`'s rule (played as `gameversion 1.9` its state differs by tic 25). With the four demos and the walk exits, the cacodemon and lost soul in E2-E4 play too. No `gameversion 1.9` route: v1.9's `A_BossDeath` (Chocolate Doom's `CheckBossEnd`: any boss on map 8 but a baron off E1) ends E2M8 and E3M8 the same with their only bosses, and plays no E4 (`registered`); `MonsterAiTests.TheBossSpecials` covers the rule. The ExM8 routes' intermissions and finales are T9.4's (`IntermissionTests` leaves every ExM8 out). `RouteChecksums` has their entries. SPEC §12 T9.2.
- [x] **T9.3 Plasma rifle and BFG.** `A_FirePlasma`, `A_FireBFG`, `A_BFGSpray` (the tracers from the shooter's angle), `A_BFGsound`, and their pickups and ammo.
  *Done when:* both weapons match vanilla on a route.
  *Done (2026-10-15):* p_pspr.c's `A_FirePlasma`, `A_BFGsound`, `A_FireBFG` (`World.Pspr.cs`) and `A_BFGSpray` (`World.Enemy.cs`: 40 aim lines from the ball's shooter over 90° around the ball's angle, an `MT_EXTRABFG` and 15d8 on each target found) ported as vanilla; the pickups, cells and `P_CheckAmmo` cases were already in (T6.1, T6.6). **Routes against vanilla, tic by tic** (`VanillaRouteTests`, on Doom II's IWAD: the same weapons, and no registered IWAD to test with): `map07-plasma` (skill 3 with monsters: pickup, a mancubus killed with fire held, the last cells into a wall, the change to the pistol) and `map24-bfg` (skill 1 with monsters: pickup with 80 cells, two shots, the player turned away while each ball flies, the second burst killing a hell knight, the change to the pistol); a damage roll changed on purpose makes `map24-bfg` differ from vanilla at tic 168. **Tests:** `WeaponTests` (the plasma rifle's 3-tic refire, cells and both flash frames; the BFG's 30-tic charge, its sound and 40 cells; the spray along the ball's angle with the shooter turned away). `StateMachineTests`' dispatch test gives the player's mobj a target for `A_BFGSpray`. No new state between tics (no save change). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (2485, 11 skipped: the OPL C harness) and `--level-check` headless on Doom II pass. SPEC §12 T9.3.
