Add a presentation event (as `P_LineAttack`'s `se_shot`) for each line that finds a target, from the shooter to it, shown with the player's tracers.
  *Done when:* a BFG burst flashes a line from the player to each target its tracers hit, under `--level-tracers=player`, and the checksums stay the same.

## M11 — Multiplayer

*Exit criterion (SPEC §11):* online co-op and deathmatch for up to 4 players in lockstep over ENet, hosted and joined from the menus; multi-player games match vanilla's netdemos tic by tic.

Each task's full text (cites, *Do:*, *Done when:*), the user's decisions, the inventory of 2026-10-16 and the order the tasks can run in (several at once) are in `docs/plans/multiplayer.md`: read the task's section there. Tick the task here.

- [x] **T11.1 N-player world and the local player.** The players in game come from the settings, `consoleplayer` becomes a per-machine index the sim's state never depends on, `GameFlow.G_Ticker` takes `ticcmd_t[]`, the checksum covers frags, cards, powers and counts, and a `NetSim` test harness runs K worlds in sync.
  *Done (2026-10-16):* **Players from the settings:** `SpawnSettings.playersingame`, a mask (bit i: player i + 1; default 1), sets `World.playeringame` in the constructor (checked: 1–15); saves keep it in their start (`SaveGameFile.VERSION` 5: older saves are refused as older). **`consoleplayer`** is a settable `World` property (0–3, default 0), not saved, not checksummed; its sim reads stay vanilla's and are per machine with nothing shared: the pickup sounds (events only), the turbo message (cleared by `HU_TakeMessages` in its tic), `wminfo.pnum`/`didsecret` and `G_WorldDone`'s `didsecret` (only the intermission reads them; commented); `idmypos` reads its sender's mobj. `P_SetupLevel`'s `viewz = 1` is every player in the game's (SPEC §12 T11.1: unobservable). **Cheats** go to their sender (`GameFlow.ST_DoCheat(player, cmd)`, `idmus`'s and `idclev`'s messages too). **`GameFlow.G_Ticker(ticcmd_t[])`**: each player in the game's cheats, its command stored in every game state (the intermission's and finale's skips see every player) and its `BTS_PAUSE`/`BTS_SAVEGAME`, as vanilla's `G_Ticker`; `G_Ticker(in ticcmd_t)` stays the single-player wrapper (this machine's specials through `G_SpecialButtons`, the command at `ConsolePlayer`); `IGameHost.G_LevelTicker` and `World.G_Ticker(in ticcmd_t)` (now a wrapper) take or build the array. **Checksum** widened (armor type, powers, cards, backpack, frags, weapons owned, maximum ammo, cheats, kill/item/secret counts, `iquehead`/`iquetail`): with the old coverage all 191 committed entries still matched, and with the new one every route, demo, intermission, finale, wipe and automap dump of DOOM1, The Ultimate Doom, Doom II (v1.9 and `final`), TNT and Plutonia still matches vanilla tic by tic (`VanillaRouteTests`, `DemoSyncTests`, …; dumps regenerated locally); `Routes/checksums.txt` rewritten (all 190 entries). **Harness:** `tests/IsoDoom.Tests/Support/NetSim.cs` (K worlds, one `consoleplayer` each, the same `ticcmd_t[]` a tic, checksums every tic, the saved state's bytes compared); `NetSimTests`: a co-op test map (CI: four starts, a DR door used by player 1, health bonuses, zombiemen and imps, a netgame-only shotgun), 2 and 4 players, vanilla and top-down tweaks, 700 tics in sync (door opened, every player's bonus, kills, the shotgun); DOOM1 E1M1 co-op, 2 and 4 players (player 1 plays `e1m1-exit`, the others a fixed pseudo-random script, god mode, skill 4), in sync for the route's 804 tics (door 4 opened, items, kills); worlds of other `consoleplayer`s equal at a level's start; a co-op save loaded on another machine going on in sync; `GameFlowTests.EveryPlayersCommandIsRead` (player 2's cheat, walk, pause and intermission skip). `dotnet build`, `dotnet test` (all WADs and vanilla dumps present: 2,828, 12 skipped: the OPL C harness and the repo-path IWAD locator; `OneMinuteOfOpl3Output` missed real time once under load and passes alone), `dotnet format --verify-no-changes` and `--level-check` headless (synthetic, DOOM1) pass. - [x] **T11.5 The lockstep protocol** (no transport; can start at once). `src/Net`: messages, lobby, `NetServer`/`NetClient`, checksum exchange, an in-memory transport, tests under latency and loss.
  *Done (2026-10-16; SPEC §12 "The lockstep protocol"):* `src/Net` (`IsoDoom.Net`, plain .NET, in `IsoDoom.sln`, referenced by the Godot project and the tests, checked by `GodotPurityTests`): `INetTransport` and `MemoryTransport` (latency, jitter, unreliable loss, reliable resends in order), the messages and their encoding with the protocol described at the top of `NetMessages.cs`, `NetServer` (lobby with the host in slot 0, hello checks, settings, ready, start, bundles in tic order, checksum windows compared, desync announced, quits) and `NetClient` (commands at clock tic + delay, bundles run when due, `TicsReady`, consistancy stamped and checked as vanilla's, the lag adjustment). `tests/IsoDoom.Tests/Net` (38 tests, 2 with DOOM1.WAD): 2–4 players over 3500 tics at 0–100 ms one way with jitter and loss, worlds checksum-equal every tic (synthetic IWAD in CI, DOOM1 E1M1 with every player and monsters), no tic run before its bundle or its clock tic, leads and traffic bounded, a forced desync announced at its window, a moved player caught by the others' consistancy check, drifting clocks (±2 %) pulled to the host's, quits, and the hello's refusals (protocol, build, IWAD, PWAD order, full, under way).
  *Note (for T11.1's merge):* the protocol's worlds put players in game by setting `World.playeringame` before the first level (`LockstepHarness.MakeWorld`); once T11.1's setting exists, the Start's player mask is what fills it, and `NetMessagesTests.Every_spawn_setting_and_tweak_travels` fails until a field T11.1 adds to `SpawnSettings` is carried by `NetWriter.Settings`/`NetReader.Settings` (bump `NetProtocol.Version`).
  *Note (for T11.6):* the default input delay can come from `NetServer.RttUsOf` (the clients report their round trips with each ping); `dev` builds all match each other at hello, so two different unstamped builds can join: consider adding the commit to the stamp. The server and clients have no timeouts of their own: ENet's peer timeout gives the `Disconnected` event that drops a player (T11.11 tunes it).
